## Introduction

Just a few days ago we have learned of the sudden departure of our dear Godbrother, His Grace Suhotra dasa Tapovanacari. He served his spiritual master faithfully and became a discerning philosopher of Vaisnava literature. In this volume, his most recent work is presented as Gaudiya Cosmotheism.

His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami, also known as Srila Prabhupada, author of 84 books on Vedic knowledge and founder of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and the Bhaktivedanta Institute desired to erect the world’s largest planetarium depicting Vedic cosmology through modern technology. He hoped that this gigantic, virtual, 3-d, animated model would give the contemporary world access to the Vedic cosmos.

To realize the vision of the Vedic planetarium, Srila Prabhupada wanted a diagram depicting the structure of the universe. To this end, he engaged devotees and artists in drawing maps according to Srimad-Bhagavatam. Simultaneously, Srila Prabhupada sent out leading students to find a competent Vedic astronomer. However, as his personal secretary Hari Sauri dasa put it, “They couldn’t find anybody who was able to do it— at least not to Prabhupada’s satisfaction.”

Nevetheless, Srila Prabhupada’s desire for the diagram was not to go unfulfilled. That elusive map was discovered by Srila Prabhupada’s disciples at the holy village of Melukote, some 50 km north of Mysore, Karnataka, India. This diagram \[see Plate 1 in this volume\] accurately depicted the entire cosmology of Srimad-Bhagavatam in an ingenious yet simple rendering. The person through whom it was revealed to the world, was the 19th century scholar-saint, Thiru-venkata Ramanuja Jeeyar Swami born in Sri Perumbudur, Tamil Nadu. Thiruvenkata Swami went on to become an acarya or spiritual leader in the preceptorial line known as the Sri-sampradaya of which Sripad Ramanujacarya is the most prominent teacher. The first chapter of this book, Sri Bhu-gola Bha-gola Visayam is a summary of Sri Thiruvenkata Ramanuja Jeeyar Swami’s work called Purana-marga Dipika. That book makes a favorable reference to another book called Bhil-Gola Nirnaya by Vedantha Desikachar. Bhu-Gola Nirnaya is also presented here in Chapter 2.

The Third Chapter comprises the verses of Bhu-gola Varnanam written by Sri Vadiraja Tirtha and translated from Sanskrit by the late V. Badarayana Murthy, of Purana Vijnana Mandira, Bangalore. Sri Vadiraja Tirtha occupies a very eminent place in the line of Madhvacarya the founder of Dvaita philosophy and he was the head of the Sode Mutt \[temple or monastery\] in the 15th and 16th Century. Sri Vadiraja Tirtha was born at Kumbhasi near Udipi in a village called Huvvina Kere in the year 1480 AD.

His philosophical commentaries included Sudha Tippani, Tat-waprakshika Tippani, Bhagavadgita Tippani, Mahabharata Tatpa-ryanirnayatika and Tantrasara Teeka. His independent works were Yuktimallika (a philosophical critical study of the Bramha sutras divided into 5 parts and 16 chapters, considered by some to be his masterpiece), Guruvrata Deepika, Prameya sangraha, Yuktimallika, Sarasa Bharati Vilasa, Pashadamatha Khandana. Other works of his included Ekadashi Nirnaya, Sankalpa Paddati, Upanyasa Ratnamala, and other stotras in Sanskrit. He also wrote the Kannada language: Tatparya Nirnaya, Vaikunta Varnane, Gundakriya, Swapna Pada, Suladhigalu, Lakshmi Shobhanepada, Bhramaragita and hundreds of Krithis in Kannada. He also wrote in the Tulu language. It may also be noted that the word Simshumara is used synonomously with the Puranic word Sisumdra referring to the

The ancient Vedic classic \[dated at two million years old\] known as the Ramayana of Valmiki contains a section on the geography of Bhu-mandala. An English translation of that portion is included in this book The elaborate description is given in the Kiskindha-Kanda by Sugriva to General Vinata in the presence of Rama and Lakshmana.

Similarly, Krishna-Dwaipayana Vyasa’s epic Mahabharata contains extensive cosmographical information in the Sabha -parva and especially within the Bhisma-parva. Indeed, it is in this book that the detailed account of our globe called Sudarsana or Bharata-khanda is written.

Therefore Bhagavatam is supposed to be the king of all Puranas." (Page 56)

The Sixth Chapter of this book represents the recent work of a gifted, young mathematician named Vamsi dasa (Chebrolu Venkata) and some of his associates. Here, they present their study of the first two chapters of the classic book on astronomy known as Surya-siddhanta. Their work is very important for it brings to light the connection between the Puranas, especially the Srimad-Bhagavatam and the Siddhantic texts. Vamsi dasa shared his findings with a world-renowned authority on Vedic astronomy, who was shocked to learn that the Srimad-Bhagavatam gave data regarding the angular velocity of the planets precisely matching that given by Surya-siddhanta. He asked to have a copy of Srimad-Bhagavatam for his own study.

In the Eighth Chapter of this book, we present an English translation of the Sanskrit work known as Virodha Mardana written by astronomer Yajnesvara, son of Sri Sakala Samvatsararya Jyotirvit Sadasiva in 1830 A.D. At our request, the learned Madhva Vaisnava scholar Sri Vishwanandana Tirtha Swamiji reviewed Yajnesvara’s work (see review below) which confirms our own views on the subject:

**Review**

*An astrologer named Yajnesvara has composed a work of the name Virodha Mardana in about seventy stanzas. He has also commented on this work himself. His aim is to remove the contradictions between astrology and the Puranas regarding cosmology. He has dealt with three aspects:*

*1.    Size of the earth.*

*2.    Shape the earth*

*3.    Lunar and solar eclipses.*

*As regarding the size of the earth, he observes that the size mentioned in Puranas is much greater than the size mentioned in Antilogy. Whereas the Puranas give a figure in lakhs of yojanas \[a lakh is 100,000 and a yojana is about eight milesj, the figure given by Surya Siddhanta in only a few thousand yojanas. The way he tries to reconcile the two is as follows; the astrologers have confined themselves to the visible earth. Actually, the earth extends to much more space. Just because we are unable to perceive these parts of the earth, it is not correct on our part to deny those parts. If the modern explorers go beyond the Polar Regions without changing the direction, they would certainly reach those lands. But, unfortunately they are unable to cross the polar regions.*

*This solves the second contradiction also. The visible smaller earth is spherical whereas the Puranic bigger earth is flat. The author staunchly supports the astrologer’s view that the earth is global. But it is funny that he tries to explain certain anomalies like the sun appearing on our head during mid-day assuming that the earth is flat. He also observes that the astrologers have made certain assumptions for the purpose of calculation. They conceive three spheric sizes—the terrestrial sphere, the cosmic sphere and the celestial sphere. It is possible that people on other parts of the bigger earth will also have their own astrological calculation suiting to their own confined smaller earth. Thus, it is not right on our part to deny the Puranic version, which is given by sage Vyasa, an incarnation of Lord Visnu.*

*Another topic dealt with by the author is how the earth is being held in space. The moderners feel that it is the gravitational force between the sun and the earth that is responsible for the stay of earth in its own orbit. The author says that instead of accepting a number of such forces between different planets and the sun, it is better to accept one divine force supporting all the planets including the earth and the sun. The modern astrologists say that the earth is going around the sun and the moon is in turn going around the earth. The author has not given any satisfactory explanation supporting the Puranic view and refuting the scientists’ view.*

*As far eclipses, he remarks that we need not necessarily accept that the eclipses are caused by the moon coming is between the sun and earth (solar eclipse) or the earth shadowing the moon (lunar eclipse). We have an alternative explanation based on the Rahu and Ketu theory. Drawing our attention to the differences in sizes of Rahu and moon, he says that although the actual sizes are different, the size of Rahu projected on the lunar orbit would match with the size of the moon. He does not explain how this is the fact.*

*Note: Neither Sheshagiri Rao of Mysore, who has gone through of this text nor 1 are satisfied with the arguments placed forward in Virodha Mardana. 1 personally feel that neither the scientist nor the traditional Puranic scholars would accept his arguments.*

In the Ninth and Tenth Chapters, we find two astronomers’ discussions of Puranic cosmology. According to Professor Christopher Minkowski, both Sisyadhivrdhida-tantra by Laila and Siddhantasekhara by Sripati are Sanskrit texts which criticize the supposed view of the Puranas that night is caused by the blocking of the sun’s rays by Meru. Although we do not support these views, still these texts are provided here without English translations as reference materials for those interested in going deeply into the subject of Vedic cosmography.

**Helio-eccentricity**

In the course of our preparation for this book, one collegue named Grantharaja dasa Brahmacari (Greg Anderson), of Bhaktivedanta Insitute, Mumbai/Berkeley made an interesting observation. He noted that the distance to the closest stars has been measured by astronomers using an effect called parallax (http://en.wikipedia.org/ wiki/Parallax). This measurement depends on the assumption of the heliocentric model because one measures the position of a star at two times while earth rotates around the sun. (Parallax can be observed with a simple example. One can see the position of one’s outstretched finger will appear to be different relative to the background when you look through your right or left eye.) For example, parallax measurements suggest that Sirius (Mrgavyadha) is about 8.6 light years away. If the stars are so far away, then they have to be irradiating light like our sun. A distance of 8.6 lights years is much larger than the diameter of the universe given in the Bhagavatam (i.e. 4 billion miles, equivalent to approx 8 light minutes).

Using parallax, one can measure the distance to only the nearby stars. For the more distant stars, astronomers have developed a scale using the “magnitude” (brightness) of the stars. But this scale depends on knowing the absolute magnitude of specific stars, whose distance has been measured by parallax. In summary, the astronomers have assumed the correctness of the heliocentric model in calculating the distances to all of the stars.

If the heliocentric model is wrong and the earth is stationary, then the parallax measurements are wrong, and the stars are much closer than the scientists think.

**Madhvacarya**

The following are Sripad Madhvacarya’s brief yet weighty summary comments (tatparyam) on the cosmological section of the Fifth Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam:

Srimad-Bhagavatam [5.18.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/18.32)

*jarayujam svedajam andajodbhidam*  
*caracaram devarsi-pitr-bhutam aindriyam*  
*dyauh kham ksitih saila-sarit-samudra-*  
*dvipa-graharksety abhidheya ekah*

**TRANSLATION: My dear Lord, You manifest Your different energies in countless forms: as living entities born from wombs, from eggs and from perspiration; as plants and trees that grow out of the earth; as all living entities, both moving and standing, including the demigods, the learned sages and the pitas; as outer space, as the higher planetary system containing the heavenly planets, and as the planet earth with its hills, rivers, seas, oceans and islands. Indeed, all the stars and planets are simply manifestations of Your different energies, but originally You are one without a second. Therefore there is nothing beyond You. This entire cosmic manifestation is therefore not false but is simply a temporary manifestation of Your inconceivable energy.**

Sripad Madhvacarya’s Comment (tatparyam)

*sarvantaryamikatvat tu*  
*sarvanama harih svayam*  
*na tu sarva-svarupatvad*  
*rupatvam upacaratah iti ca*

**The Lord Hari Himself is called Sarvanama (He who is the source of all names) because of being the Supersoul of all, and not because of being the personal form of all. His being the form \[of everything\] is a figurative expression.**

Srimad-Bhagavatam [5.19.31](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/19.31)

*evam tava bharatottama jambudvipa-varsa-vibhago yathopadesam upavarnita iti.*

**TRANSLATION: My dear King Pariksit, O best of the descendants of Bharata Maharaja, I have thus described to you, as I myself have been instructed, the island of Bharata-varsa and its adjoining islands. These are the islands that constitute Jambudvipa.**

Sripad Madhvacarya’s Comment (tatparyam)

*anadhikarino devah svarga-stha bharatodbhavam*  
*vancchanty atma-vimoksartham udrekarthe ‘dhikarinah iti kaurme*

**Those demigods that are residents of the heavenly planets but are not occupying posts desire to be born on Bharata-varsa for the purpose of liberation. Those who occupy posts strive for opulence.**

## Chapter 1 - Sri Bhu-gola Bha-gola Visayam

Obeying the commands of the Supreme Lord, the creator Brahma created everything in this universe, strictly following the pattern of things as in the previous cycle (Kalpa). Again, at the behest of the Lord, he created the nine Prajapatis. These Prajapatis and several other sages had direct experience of the globe (Bhugola) and the celestial bodies (Bhagola). Some of these sages directly acquired this information by the power of Yoga. Some of them could, by the power of their penance, suitably changing their physical bodies to suit the various regions, travel to those regions using Gulika, aeroplanes etc, to visit those regions and get first-hand experience of those regions and celestial bodies.

Similarly, information about these is available in Ramayana, Mahabharata, Srimadbhagavatam, Sri Vishnupurana, Matsya, Kurma and Varaha Puranas etc. The same is available in Veda, Vedanta and scriptures dealing with God, communicated to the creator by the Supreme Lord himself. Similarly, ample information is available in the eight theories propagated by Brahma, Vyasa and Surya etc, at the behest of the Lord, which could forecast the movements of the celestial bodies, the eclipses and so on. Similarly, information about these is available in Jataka, Skanda, Garga Samhita and others. Several devices developed based on Surya-siddhanta and so on further examined the information available in these texts. All this information was collected and collated by the great Sri Tiru-vengada Ramanuja Jeeyar Swami with the honorities etc, who was an outstanding scholar in all the scriptures. All this information is given in detail without giving any room for doubts or misapprehension in the work Puranamargadipika authored by this great Acharya for the easy comprehension of all.

We shall now give a brief account of the enormous information provided in Puranamargadipika, which abounds in the authoritative quotations taken from Smrtis, Itihasas, Puranas and the theories of Vyasa, Brahma and Surya etc. We have provided a sketch of the globe and the celestial bodies in this book.

According to that, there are fourteen worlds, seven islands and seven oceans. Among these, the Jambu Island is at the centre. At its centre is the mount Meru. To its south are situated the three Varsas viz. Bharatavarsam, Kimpurushavarsam, Kuruvarsam and Ilavarsam surrounding Meru. To its east is Bhadrasvavarsam. To the west is the Ketumalavarsam.

Among them, Bharatavarsa is described thus in Vishnupurana. In the third chapter of second Amsa of Sri Vishnupurana, it is stated that, that region which is to north of the sea and that which is to the south of Himalaya Mountains is called Bharatavarsa, where there are the descendents of Bharati. O, Great Sage! The dimension of this is 9,000 yojanas. This is the place of action (Karmabhumi) for those, who are destined to go to the Heaven and Vaikuntha. “Now listen to the nine parts of the Bharatavarsa. This Bharatavarsa is divided into nine khandas (parts) as, Indra dvipa, Kaseru, Tamraparna, Gabhastiman, Nagadvipa, Saumyadvipa, Gandharva, Varuna and this is the ninth Dvipa, surrounded by sea”. The statements like these divide the Bharatavarsa measuring 1,000 Yojanas, divided into nine continents. Amongst them, Bharatadvipa (Indian Continent) is situated to the north of the salty ocean (dug out by the King Sagara) and to the south of the Himalaya Mountain. For the people, who are desirous of reaching Heaven or Vaikuntha or the celestial region, the means of action and inaction can be adopted in this place. In Sri Ramayana’s Kiskindhakanda, Ninth Sarga, by the statement.

It is said that, the eyes of all the living beings and the light emanating from the Sun etc, is effective in the Sudarshanadvipa only. In the fifth chapter of Jambukhanda Parva, being part of Sri Bhishmaparva of Mahabharata, it is said:

This Bharatakhanda is called Sudarshanadvipa, since it looks beautiful to the eyes of the onlookers. Being circular, it looks like the disc of the Lord and it is attached to the cyclic time in the form of a disc presided over by the God Sudarshana. It is in the form of a globe since all the four corners of this Bharatakhanda are rounded like the bael fruit. The nature of this Sudarshanadvipa or Bharatavarsa is viewed from the moon. Half of this earth of Bharatavarsa appears like the rabbit and a small pipal-leaf, the other half appears in the form of a big pipal, and they are surrounded by all varieties of vegetation.

In this Bharatavarsa, the rest of the portions other than the portions that appear like the rabbit and the pipal-leaf are almost covered by water except a few small portions of earth.

The head portion and the body portion are said to be the southern and northern continents and as stated (sanskrit)

The Naga and Kasyapa dvipas form the two ears, Tamraparna appears to be the head, Malayaparvata as neck. Thus, the southern continent is described. The entire northern part/continent is said to be the body of this rabbit. Now, in this Bharatavarsa the one half in the form of rabbit and the small leaf, representing the head of the rabbit along with the two ears is called Africa. The front leg protion close to the neck is called Europe. The body portion is called Asia. The small pipal-leaf portion is called Australia. The other half portion of the earth looking like the big pipal-leaf is said to South America and North America. These are the names given by foreigners (Hunas).

In the sixth chapter of Jambukhanda Parva forming a portion of Bhishmaparva in Mahabharata, it is said that the affluence of both divine and human of those continents seems to be manifold. Hence, except for the peoples of Bharatavarsa, the wealth of people residing in the other varsas, though human, is really similar to the wealth of divine beings. Those varsas are no doubt beyond our comprehension. In spite of this, we should accept that, the information available about these Varsadvipas in the scriptures as truth.

In the 17th chapter of fifth book of [Sribhagavatam](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/17), it is said - “Even in this Jambudvipa only Bharatavarsa is the place meant for rituals and sacrifices. The other eight Varsas are the places to enjoy the remaining results of good deeds of the people, who were in the Heaven. They are mentioned as the Heaven on earth”.

Accordingly, in this Jambudvipa, except Bharatavarsa, all the other eight continents are said to be the places of enjoyment of the remnants of the results of the good deeds of those who were in the Heaven earlier. In the second chapter of second part of Sri Vishnu-purana, as it is said -

**“Oh Sage? Those are the heavenly abodes on earth of the pious people, who were in the Heaven. The sinners do not enter into these regions even after hundreds of lives. ”**

Except the Indian continent, all the other continents are the heaven on earth. Sinners who have not done pious deeds cannot enter into these places, even if they put forth efforts in hundreds of lives. The heavenly regions though existing in the sky, are not visible, because they are made up of subtle elements, which are transparent. Because of this, they are not capable of obstructing the view of stars etc. in the sky. This spherical Bharatavarsa is held by the will and nature of the Lord of the universe, as he is the substratum of all. In the Puranas, the spherical form of the earth is denied just because it refers to the earthly regions, which are not covered by water, but not the regions including those which are totally covered by water (i.e. entire globe along with the watery portions is in the form of a sphere only).

In the 12th chapter of Jnanapada, the portion dealing with knowledge, of the divine Padma Samhita, it is said - “By this cosmic egg, all the regions are covered. These regions are situated one over the other looking like the Kapittha fruit.” The spherical regions covered more by lands, covered more by water, covered more by light, more by air and more by ether, are situated one over the other, like the Kapittha fruits kept one over the other and are innumerable.” As far the greater earth, there is the Meru Mountain at the centre, there is a small Meru at the naval of this Bharatavarsa. The great snowy mountains like Kailasa etc., the abodes of Sankara, Kubera and others are not visible to us. The snowy mountain visible to us is a small portion of a far greater snowy mountain, and hence, associated with all its characteristics of that and called by the same name. This is a very small mountain. There is Lanka at a slight distance in the salt sea.

Further from this, there is a mountain called Puspitaka stretching into the skies with its golden and silver peaks in which the Sun actually moves in line with the golden peak, during Dakshinayana. Then, there are mountains called Kunjaparvata, Agastyabhavana, Bhogapati, Rsyan. These are visible only to the divine beings, but not for others. In this way, it is described in Ramayana.

In Mahabharata, it is said that, in the Jambudvipa, on the other side of this Himavan Mountain, there is a place where the divine sculptor Maya went and collected some stones and built a beautiful palace in this Bharatakhanda, visible to everybody. But, this palace although seen well by all the pious people, was not visible to the certain Kings, who were not good at heart, in spite of there severe efforts to see. This reminds us of the earthly heavens mentioned above. There is no dearth of such examples.

If it is asked that, is non-perception of these regions because they are the habitations of the divine beings? If this is the reason for their non-visibility, then how come the habitations of the divine places, namely the orb of the moon etc,, are visible even to the sinners? Should they not be invisible to the sinners?

This objection is answered thus - certain astro-bodies in the sky are meant for indicating the time for the performance of duties in this Karmabhumi (land where one has to perform the rituals prescribed in the shastras). They also put an end to the sins of those who view them. They have been created only with the purpose of their vision, even to the sinners. The other continents have been created not with this purpose, but only for the enjoyment of the inhabitants. Hence, for persons who are not endowed with such good deeds these regions are not visible. While criticizing the Puranas, one should know that all the views given by the Puranics are in accordance with the different traditions followed in these Puranas. Moreover, anyone venturing to condemn those views should comprehend their views and then criticize. If one clearly understands those views, there is no room for criticism. Without studying those views, if one criticizes he will become a laughing stock.

There are things, which are perceptible and imperceptible. People who have perceived certain parts of the perceptible things, have written the Puranas. If there are no contradictions in the views expressed in the Puranas, then even the views, expressed regarding imperceptible things hold good, when there are no contradictions. It is in the fitness of things to accept these.

Due to the passage of time and change of places, the things such as residence, lands etc., seen in this world, attain certain changes, get different names and forms. Similarly, the different regions divided by the old sages like Kasyapa Prajapati, the monarch Prthu and others, and other rivers, rivulets, lakes, ponds and other gulfs etc, the different mountains, gardens etc., attained new names and forms which are different from their previous names and forms. If people without knowing this, begin to laugh at these things, become themselves a laughing stock.

As human beings attain different changes and states in their nature, even in this globe and planetary changes will take place due to passage of time. In Suryasiddhanta, it is clearly said that, noting down the changes that take place from time to time, one should take corrective measures in calculating their movements.

As there is, change in the standard measurement and changes also take place in different kalpas, taking into account. The increase or decrease in things, the expanse of the cosmic egg is mentioned variedly as 50 crores, becomes 100 crores of Yojanas is mentioned differently.

Mutual contradictions are not there in the statements of different Puranas as the Puranas describe the globe and the planetary system, as created differently in different kalpas, since the creation differs from Kalpa to Kalpa.

There are certain doubts regarding the reply given by certain sages, regarding the Kings and monarchs, who passed away long ago, by their contemporary sages, when they were questioned by the people, who were living long after them. The souls that take birth as Kings and monarchs in these different Kalpas are no doubt different. However, these positions (like the position of Indra, Brahma, etc.) can be occupied only by such souls who have done certain specific good deeds. Since, they occupy the same specific positions, which are based on similar good deeds in different Kalpas; they are called by the same names. The histories of people in the previous Kalpas narrated to the people in the succeeding Kalpas may change slightly, but there is no room for any doubt.

There is a statement in Brahmasiddhanta, “the earth is said to be having an expanse of 50 crores (Yojanas). This is purely an imagination. This is done with a purpose of creating distress and detachment in the minds of Kings, who are infatuated by being the rulers of a small kingdom.” As per this, some people say that, this statement mentioning that, the different parts of the earth measures more than 50 crore Yojanas, is just to create a sense of distress and detachment in the minds of Kings, infatuated by becoming Lords of several regions. This is not valid.

Because, if this is the reason to mention a false statement, then it is better to mention like the Advaitins, that all these kingdoms directly perceived are just like the kingdom seen in dreams. This statement is more conducive to create detachment in the Kings, rather than the previous one. On the other hand, the previous statement creates more greed in the Kings to acquire even those regions. This can be further clarified by an incident. Suppose somebody falsely says that, there is a treasure trove in a particular house, and then the greedy owner of the house sacrifices a human being, demolishes the house, and tries to acquire the treasure trove in vain. Then, he becomes enraged as he could not find the treasure and tries to chase the person who gave the false statement, with an intention to kill him. Similarly, if it is said that, there are several other regions to be conquered, then greedy kings, even after the performance of several sacrifices, when, not able to find those regions, burn all the Puranas and also try to search for the creator and others who gave this false statement, to kill them. Hence, no one should say that Brahma gave a false statement.

By giving a wrong interpretation to a small shloka and claiming on that basis that, the views of Sruthis, Smrtis, Itihasas and Puranas are false, is only a deed of indiscrimination. Hence, this should not be done. While walking a long distance to reach a village of course, we tell young kids, who resist to walk a long distance, that the distance to be covered in ten hours can be covered in a very small time (say ^ an hour). Similarly, when Brahma mentions that, there are wonderful earthly regions the expanse of which is 50 crores of yojanas, people who hesitate to calculate things according to mathematics, will begin to calculate, being lured by these regions. Hence, it should not be stated that Brahma gave a false statement.

Whatever he has mentioned is truth, the reasons for these are as follows -

In the Purana, there are statements regarding the greater earth. We should not exclude the different Dvipas (Islands) and oceans included here. In Brahma, Surya and Vyasa Siddhantas, the earthly globe included in the midst of the solar system (Jyotiscakra) consists of two parts, the Southern Hemisphere and the Northern Hemisphere. In the Southern Hemisphere, Dvipas and oceans are seen. In the northern Hemisphere, there is said to be Jambudvipa in which is situated the nine Khandas and nine Varsas. If the fact is that, there are Varsas and Dvipas, then why the statement that, they are included in this northern hemisphere? Is this not a false statement? The answer is, subtle parts of dvipas and oceans are included in this. Hence, it is not a false statement.

The other Varsas and Dvipas are not places meant for rituals and sacrifices. Hence, there is no change of time in those regions as Krta, Treta etc. Only Bharatavarsa is the region where the changes of time in Krita, Treta etc. could be felt and hence, it is Karmabhumi (place for performance of rituals). Bharatavarsa is the object of the theories of Astronomy, as they prescribe the specific times for the performance of the sacred duties like rituals and sacrifices. This is the sum and substance of the shastras.

The cycle/circle of time in which the Sun is the center (naval), daily goes around the mountain Manasottara, every day starting from the western direction (clockwise). The stars and planets in that cycle, based on their axis, rotate according to their own measurements in the eastern direction (anticlockwise).

Whether the earth is static or not is the next question. But, the perceptual experience is that the earth is static. For a person, who is moving in a ship, the shore appears to be moving, but really it is not so. Any amount of effort to see that, the earth is moving is in vain, because it is not moving.

If it is argued that, the earth is rotating then one should take it for granted that, the sky regions including the circle of the clouds and the orb of the moon are also rotating along with the earth. Instead of accepting all these regions that are rotating along with the earth, it is better to accept the earth is static and this is in accordance with the shastras.

Many astronomers of yore have calculated the eclipses, the movements of the planets and stars and also the elliptical movements taking the stand that, the earth is static. These calculations have been proved to be right.

In the cycle of time, the Sun is the centre (naval). All the planets are established in this circle. The clockwise cyclic movement of the Sun at the centre creates the movements of the entire planetary system and it depends upon the distance of each planet to the Sun. This has been discussed clearly in the Purvamimamsa in the Adhikarana viz Lokaveda while discussing the interpretation of the scriptural statement,

Here, quoting the view of Somanatha, it is said that the earth moves in all the three worlds. Vedanthadeshika refuses this view in the sloka of Tatvamuktakalapa viz Aryabhata accepted the view that, the earth moves round the Sun, only to facilitate easy calculation. But, it is not correct to establish this view.

The will of God to create this universe does not depend upon the lighter way of our logic. Hence, the view based on the felicity of the logic should not be taken into account.

In the world it is seen that one stitches a hat according to the measurement of the head—not that one removes some portions of the head to adjust to the measurement of the hat. Accordingly, we should develop mathematical calculations in accordance with the creation of the Lord. This alone is the right way.

If a person creates a machine (mechanical device) and hides the secrets of that machine, one has to understand the intricacies of that machine only by consulting him. Similarly, when we try to understand the intricacies of the imperceptible things created by the Lord, we can do so only by understanding the Sanskrit Vedantic texts, which reveal the intention of the Lord and which are created by him. Here, logic is of no use.

The contemporary Huns (foreigners) cannot see and comprehend the globe (Bhumandala) the expanse of which is 50 crores of yojanas. Similarly, they cannot understand the varsas and dvipas, and the rivers, mountains and cities accommodated there, the people living there. The lower lakes (regions) like Atala and others, the inhabitants like the demons therein. They cannot understand at the height of 20,000 Yojanas from the earth, the movement of the planet viz Megha which creates rain, fire, at the height of 30,000 Yojanas, the movement of the planet siddha and the people moving above that region. Similarly, it is not possible for them to understand the nine planets, which foretell the good..., etc. and their official positions indicating their experience, sport etc., their specific forms, the subplanets like Dhumapata, the Matrgrahas (planets), which afflict the bodies, the demonic planets causing troubles to the human beings, the Dhumaketus portending the sufferings at specific times, the planets foretelling the good...in the future, which are located by the side of the Sun and the Moon, but not generally visible, but visible only at certain times, the upper regions like Heaven and so on, because they are like those persons who have generated and seen Indra etc, in those regions by virtue of their penance and Yoga.

Besides, apart from the Sun, the other celestial bodies cannot be accepted as planets. They are located above the Sun and below the pole-star viz. Dhruva. Each one of them is luminous body and in each one of them, there Vimanas (domes), Mantapas, Forts, Cities and so on. Only blessed people live along with their retinue in those places. They are innumerable. The great sages did not mention about the measurements etc of those, because the Jyotishi or Astrologer is meant only for the calculation time for the proper performance of duties and rituals. This time for performance only depends upon the movements of the nine planets. This is the reason for not discussing other celestial bodies located above the Sun.

The constellations like Ashvini and others and the seven sages etc., move on their axis in the celestial path (Vayumarga), with their own individual speed as per the dictates of the Lord. The planetary positions mentioned in the Puranas are the positions of the demigods.

Without understanding that, the planetary positions and positions of the Mandalas (systems) are different from those, criticizing these only amounts to indiscrimination. The Mandalas in which there is the movement of planets in different Rashis is possible, alone are visible to us. The planets responsible for the change of time, presiding over these Mandalas are not visible to us. Just because, they are not visible, it is not correct to say that they do not exist.

Besides, in the Harivamsa etc.' the following information is given. The Sun shines forth with the flames of fire of the Agnihotra (ritual). The presiding officer of this orb of the Sun is the sun-god. The inner reality of this Mandala is the Supreme Lord. There is also information about the chariot of the Sun,...the cyclic time, the activities of the Sun. All these are explained individually. There is a special description of the nature and characteristics of the Sun caused by the enriching of the tawny cloud (Aruna Megha). The planets and stars with their natural effulgence derive more effulgence from the effulgence of the Sun. The Sun is the sustainer of the World, as he creates the rains, which are the mainstay for all the animate beings and for the crops giving food. The base for the Sun is Dhruva. The base for Dhruva is the Simsumaracakra. The base for this is Lord Narayana. The world of the Moon (Chandra) is situated above the Sun. Only one digit (Kala) of this Moon appeared when the milky ocean was churned and Rudra decorated his head with this digit. When Atri, the great sage, was in deep meditation of Brahman, the nectarean water, which was the material cause of the orb of Moon, oozed out of his eyes. Then it spread all over the ten directions illuminating them. Then, the presiding all these ten directions tried their level best to hold them, but they were not able to do so. Then it fell on the earth. Then, the four-faced creator, who was witnessing all these things, made this watery body to move round the earth for its well-being. The Sun is responsible for the increase of the nectar and effulgence of the Moon. The digits increasing or decreasing on account of the increase or decrease of nectar, are visible to us. The reason is his monthly movement on his axis under the Sun.

The shadow of the moon named Gajacchaya presided over by Rahu is the cause for the Solar Eclipse. The shadow of the worlds in the orb of the Moon is seen to us as the black spot in the moon. In the Puranas it is said that the moon disappears in the herbs and waters during the new-moon day. This refers only to the presiding deity of the orb of the moon, but not the orb of the moon itself. The Rahu-mandala is in between Guru and Sani and is invisible. This Rahu is a planet in the form of shadow. Hence, he utilizes the shadows of the Sun and the Moon. With equal power he utilizes the shadows of the both and creates Solar and Lunar eclipses.

When we compare the information given in the Puranas by the sages and saints, who have created and seen the Sun and the Moon and the information discovered by spending enormous amount of money by the foreigners, the information given by the foreigners appears to be scanty and unclear. The nature and characteristics of the celestial bodies such as Grahamandalas and Nakshatras which are situated at a long distance cannot be seen and described by persons who are using telescopes, where as the sages and saints residing in those areas, who are the creators and actual viewers of these can do so. People who have superficially viewed and understood some of the characteristics, should not criticize the views of those, who have created and experienced the characteristics of these directly. If they criticize, it accounts to ignorance.

In Sri Ramayana, it is said that, when two birds viz Sampati and Jatayu with a desire to enter into the orb of the Sun, were flying at great heights, they saw the earth from that height. Then, they could not see the human beings, animals and birds. The great mountains appeared like beds of rocks, the rivers appeared like threads, the great forests appeared like lawns, great cities appeared like the wheels of a cart, and when they reached further heights, even these things disappear and only the earth was visible.

**Sri Conclusion**

Foreign researchers mention this, as one of the planets along with the Sun etc. People in this world, from the monarch to the ordinary householder, are enjoyers of this world, according to their own karma. Accordingly, they have several things to enjoy, instruments to enjoy and places of enjoyment. Thus, they are witnessed directly by great people of discrimination. Such people will not feel that, the places, which are several thousand miles away from this earth, which are far greater in dimension, and full of effulgence, most beautiful on account of the presence of locations, where rivers and mountains are created. Such are the orbs of the Sun, Moon and so on. The Divine beings inhabiting these regions are invisible to us, because, our sights are limited and even the instruments we use are far inferior. Just because of this, wise people will not feel that, statements made in the Puranas by persons who have created and directly perceived these celestial objects as true statements. In this physical world, we have seen that even people with very little wealth have several places for their enjoyment and experience. They have got the required devices and also people who can make them enjoy these things.

Wise people do not doubt the information given in the Puranas. Sage Saubhari, who had performed very little penance, was able to obtain fifty bodies by the power of his penance and performed various activities. Such being the case, persons like the Creator— Brahma, Rudra, Indra, Surya-Sun, etc, who have performed greater penance, can obtain any number of bodies and perform various activities with much ease. Hence, wise people need not doubt the veracity of the truth contained in this information. People who have comprehensive knowledge of this will simply accept these aspects mentioned in this work.

In the scriptures, it has been stated that, the Lord of the Universe created certain great souls, such as Brahma, Rudra, Indra, Sun etc. who have performed severe penance, and gave them the sovereignty over different regions and worlds. It is just as a King bestows certain regions and powers to certain people who have taken refuge in him, as per their desires. Similarly, the Lord has also bestowed sovereignty upon certain regions to the persons, who have taken refuge in him, according to their efforts. Some people become suppliant to those rulers and get certain benefits. Similarly, some people take refuge in Brahma, Rudra and Surya etc, and have their desires fulfilled. In this region, it is seen that according to one’s own Karma, certain persons are considered to be auspicious and certain other are considered to be inauspicious. Similarly, in the celestial region too, God has created certain planets that are considered to be auspicious and inauspicious. Those people who have personally witnessed these planets in their own regions, accept this as a fact.

There are certain people in this world, who do not accept the existence of the different Varshas, Dvipas, Oceans and the different lakes, the rulers of the regions like the Caturmukha Brahma, rulers of different Mandalas like the Sun, etc, just because they cannot be perceived by the ordinary eye. If this is the case, then we have to conclude that, even the Lord who has created this whole universe is non-existent—resulting in atheism. Then, as a consequence of this, we have to accept the views of modern atheists also.

The Lord, who is the inner soul of everything, has assigned these subtle physical bodies to them, according to their past karma. This should not be neglected and we have to see that the beings do not come into existence on their own and there is an absolute necessity to accept a creator for the whole Universe.

On the other hand, the believers or the theists, accept both perceptible and imperceptible things, the Lord and his wonderful creation. The view of the atheists who accept that everything comes into existence on its own, cannot be likened to this view. Let us not accept the existence of a potter, but keep all the ingredients of the pot like water, mud and the instruments and similarly, in the absence of a weaver, keep all the materials required for the production of a cloth, and just see whether pot or cloth will come into existence on their own?! It is highly impossible. Hence, we cannot accept the theory of the modern scientists.

The physical body of the human being is a wonderful creation, which is undergoing some change or the other, every moment. Suppose, we accept that, this body is created by our parents, then, suppose, one little finger is lost in this body, are the parents or any other individual capable of recreating this finger, as it was originally? It is impossible. Hence, we have to accept a Lord, who is omniscient and omnipotent and the scriptures revealing His intentions.

Then, why do not we accept Mohammedans and Christians? This can be answered as follows -

In those scriptures they have not clearly defined the nature, possession, forms and activities of the Lord as it exists. Regarding the question of diversity in creation, they have not given satisfactory answers. Further, just like the urchins they claim that, this world was created 6,000 years before! Before that, this world was not in existence! The deluge will take place shortly. In the earth and the celestial region, only things that can be perceived by the natural eye alone are existent. Then, we get the following doubts: whether God existed before 6,000 years or not? Even if He existed, whether He was capable of creating this world or not? Since, this view of the modernists creates so many confusions, we have to discard these views and accept the views of our predecessors, which are as follows -

The Primordial Matter is unlimited. In this, God has created innumerable and infinite cosmic eggs, which are like the Kapitta fruits, containing innumerable objects or experiences and their instruments according to the past Karma of the inhabitants of these places, like the seed and its germination. The eternal Lord makes these continuously like the flow of water. Although He is all pervasive, He resides in His eternal abode, viz. Srivaikunta, to facilitate the individual souls to attain Him in that celestial world. It is also necessary to accept the scriptures written in Sanskrit (Vedas only) as the authority, which are given by the God out of His compassion. These scriptures describe His nature, form and wealth and also the nature of the individual soul and means of final emancipation. All these are clearly established in the work Puranamargadipika of Tiruvenata Ramanuja Jiyar with valid proofs, reasoning and Aitihyas (oral traditions).

## Chapter 2 - Bhu-gola Nirnaya By Vedantha Desikachar

**Verse 1:**

![рис-017.jpg|505](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-017--56a458418da5cc3a.jpg>)

**The surface of the earth is 50 crore yojanas. It is like this Jambu Dwipa is 100,000 yojanas wide encircled by an ocean of equal width compared to the salt ocean and it is encircled by the sugarcane juice ocean of the same width. The next islands and oceans are both twice wider than the ocean.**

![рис-018.jpg|516](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-018--afbd5e923613dc7f.jpg>)

Commentary: The thickness of the shell of the Brahmanda is one crore yojanas. The cavity within the shell is 50 crore yojanas both from top to bottom and from side to side. The seven worlds—Bhu, bhuvah, suvah, Mahah, janah, Tapah and satya are situated there. The earth is 50 crore yojanas wide. The break-up is like this: Jam-buDwipa is 100,000 yojanas wide, there are nine varshas there. They are: Bharata, Kimjpurasha, Harivarsha, Ilavrta, Ramyaka, Hiranvat, Kuru, Bharadvarsha and Ketumala. The outstanding mountains of these varshas are Himavan, Hemakuta, Nishada, Nila, Shveta, Srhga, and Himavan, Hemakuta, Nishadha lie to the south of Meru: Nila, Shveta and Srhga lie to north of Meru: Malya-van is to the east and Gandhamadana to the west of Meru. The six mountains excepting Malyavan and Gandhamadana are 2,000 yojanas high and 2,000 yojanas wide. The first mentioned six mountains stretch from east to west of them Nila and Nishadha are 100,000 yojanas long, Hemakuta and Shveta are 90,000 yojanas long and Himavan and Srhga are 80,000 yojanas long and Himavan and Srhga are 80,000 yojanas long. This difference in length is because Jambu Dwipa is circular. Malyavan Gandhamadana stretch from Nila to Nishadha and are 24,000 yojanas high. Among the varshas, Bharata, Kimpursha and Hari-varsha lie to the south of Meru, Ramyaka Hiranvat and Kuru lie to the north of Bhadrashva lies to the east and Ketumala lies to the west of Meru. Ilavrta is at the centre. Meru is situated at the centre of Ilavrata. It is 84,000 yojanas high and 16,000 yojanas part has gone into the earth at the bottom in Ilavrta. Ilavrta-varsha is a square of 34,000 yojanas side. It lies between Nila and Nishadha. The other six varshas are 9000 yojanas wide each. This is from north to south. Bhadrashava-varsha stretches from east to West. The distance between Malyan and the ocean is 32,000 yojanas. The stretch from Nila to Nishadha measures 34,000 yojanas. Similarly, the distance between Gandhamadana and the ocean (on the left side) is 32,000 yojanas. The stretch (of Ketumala) from Nila to Nishadha measures 34,000 yojanas. The other six varshas are earth 9,000 yojanas wide. This is from south to north considering from east to west, all the six varshas touch the ocean at both sides. This is their east-west stretch.

Thus, Jambudwipa, Bhadrashva and Ketumala together account for 64,000 yojanas. Ilavrta is 34,000 yojanas. Malyavan and Gandhamadana are each 2,000 yojanas thick. This makes up the 100,000 yojanas of Jambudvipa. Considering the south-north direction, the six varshas account for 54,000 yojanas. Ilavrta accounts for 34,000 yojanas. The six mountains 2,000 yojanas thick each account for a total of 12,000 yojanas. This makes up the 100,000 yojanas of Jambu Island. Jambu Dwipa is encircled by the salt ocean equally wide. Salt ocean is encircled by Plaksha Island 200,000 yojanas wide Plaksha Island is encircled by the sugarcane juice ocean which is also 200,000 yojanas wide. Like this, the next islands Shalmali, Kusha, Kraunch, Shaka and Puskara should each be understood as twice broader than the previous ones.

**Verse 2:**

![рис-019.jpg|500](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-019--92a9404763b2ca45.jpg>)

**The Island Jambu, Plaksha, Shalmali, Kusha, Krauncha Shaka and Puskara are encircled by the salt ocean, sugarcane juice ocean, wine ocean, ghee ocean, yogurt ocean, milk ocean and the pure water ocean respectively.**

Commentary: In the five islands starting from Plaksha, there are seven mountains touching the ocean on both sides. These divide the islands into seven parts, Puskara Island is like a ring. It is divided into two circular parts by Manasottara Mountain which runs in the middle of the island throughout. It is 50,000 yojanas high and equally wide. So on one side of Meru the oceans account for 127 lakh yojanas. The distance between the pure water ocean and the ultimate end of the share should be regarded as 253.5 lakh yojanas.

Like this, the total width of the seven islands and the seven oceans becomes 507 lakh yojanas.

**Verse 3:**

![рис-020.jpg|515](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-020--663e0aa7d53aad95.jpg>)

*saptadvipam sasindhu dvigunatavitataya svarnabhumya paritam*

**The seven islands and oceans are encircled by kanchana-bhumi which is twice in length to the former.**

*lokalokaadrina saapyaayutavitatina tavaducchennavita*

**The kancana-bhumi is encircled by lokaloka which is 10,000 yojanas high and equally wide.**

*lokalokatcha meror bhavati hi dharani yavate dhwantabhumya*

**The width of the region of darkness is equal to the distance between Meru and Lokaloka.**

*tavatya asau vrtah syat udakaparidhina sa api vedondabhittya*

**The Lokaloka is encircled by a sheet of water. This sheet of water is encircled by shell of the Brahmanda.**

Commentary: These Islands and oceans are encircled by Kanchana Bhumi is encircled by Kanchana Bhumi which is twice in width to the combined width of the islands and oceans. This Kanchana Bhumi is encircled by the mountain Lokaloka which is 10,000 yojanas high and equally wide. So the total distance between Meru and Lokaloka on either side is 1,267.5 lakh yojanas. Lokaloka is also surrounded by the region of darkness on all sides: The width of this region also is equal to the distance between Meru and Lokaloka on one side. Beyond the region of darkness there is the shell of the Brahmanda.

In this way, the distance between Meru and the shell on either side is 2,523 lakh yojanas. Of that 23 lakh yojanas constitute the width of solid water (Ghanodaka). 2,500 lakh yojanas constitute the width of the earth. It should be understood in the same way on all the four sides and thus the total width of the earth is 5,000 lakh or 50 crore yojanas.

Like this, the cavity within the Brahmanda measures 50 crore yojanas on all sides.

**Verse 4:**

![рис-021.jpg|494](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-021--cf06acdabd4d300e.jpg>)

*saptadvipam samudraih saha muniniyataih kotibhih panchab-hih syat*  
*lokalokadi meruvantaramapi ravibhih kotibhih saptashashtya*  
*lakshaih panchayataiah syadatha sharanayanaih kotibhih bhuta ramaih*  
*lakshaih syat charka valantaramamanashikhar-yanada bhit-togoh cha madhyam !!*

**The total width of the seven islands and oceans is 570 lakh yojanas. The distance between Meru and Lokaloka is 1267.5 yojanas... Like this, the earth is 25 crore yojanas on all sides of Meru.**

**Verse 5:**

![рис-022.jpg|516](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-022--80e0090997828d40.jpg>)

*sharanetrakotibhih bhuparidhijalam banavahni-lakshaih*  
*syat/abhito merorevam panchashatkotiyojana bhumih !!*

Commentary: This earth is 70,000 yojanas high. There are seven Netherworlds below the earth. They are like holes. They are Ata-la, Vitala, Nitala, Gathastiman, Mahatala, Sutala and Patala. Each one of them is 9,000 yojanas high. Between them and below the earth also there is a partition of the earth each 1,000 yojanas thick. Below all these, lives the Shesha Ioka, (The world of the serpent Sesha). Below this there is Garbhodaka. The hells are also situated there. The total height of all these is slightly more than 24.98 crore yojanas.

This has been stated in accordance with Bhagavata where it is stated that the distance between Surya and the shell of Brahmanda is 25 crore yojanas.

**Verses 6 & 7:**

![рис-023.jpg|523](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-023--fc1dab5161b02359.jpg>)

**The disc of the sun is 100,000 yojanas above the earth. The earth itself is 70,000 yojanas high. Thus, the remaining nether worlds should be placed below the earth. That Surya is situated one lakh yojanas above the earth is already stated. This is called Bhuvarloka Chandra and is situated one lakh yojanas above Chandra. Budha is two lakh yojanas above the star belt. Sukra is two lakh yojanas above Budha. Kuja is situated two lakh yojanas above Budha. Brhaspati is situated two lakh yojanas above Budha. Shani is situated two lakh yojanas above Brhaspati. The seven sages (the great bear) are situated one lakh yojanas above the sun’s disc until Dhruvaloka. This is called svarloka, the total height of which is 14 lakh yojanas.**

**The area above Saptarshi-mandala until Dhruva is called a Vish-nuloka. Maharloka is situated above Dhruva-mandala and it is one crore yojanas high. Janaloka is above that and it is two crore yojanas high. Tapoloka is situated above Janaloka and it is eight crore yojanas high. Satyaloka is situated above Janaloka and it is twelve crore yojanas high. So from Dhruva to the top-end of Satyaloka it will be twenty-three crore yojanas. Above that, the region of darkness is slightly less than two crore yojanas. Beyond that, is the shell of the Brahmanda. Like this the horizontal and vertical distance between the sun and the shell of the Brahmanda is twenty five crore yojanas. This fact is established.**

## Chapter 3 - Bhu-gola Varnanam By Sri Vadiraja Tirtha

**INVOCATION (Verses 1-8)**

I bow down to the feet of Lord Narayana who appears with His Horse-headed form (Hayagriva), who is both far and near and is) dear to those who always remember Him. He is not fully comprehended even by the Upanisads. He has afforded lodging in His heart to Kamaladevi (Sri Maha Laksmi) who knows Him and yet knows Him not. He is verily the lake wherein the fish-like eyes of adoring devotees move about sportively. He is indeed Lord Hayagriva. I bow down to Sri Vyasamaharsi, who is the sun that dispels the darkness of our hearts. He is the originator of all the sacred sciences. He is none other than Lord Sri Narayana, the Creator of all. Lord Vedavyasa is like the thunderbolt that strikes at those who choose the wrong path and mislead others.

My salutations to Sridevi, the highest in intellect and the model of modesty, but fearless at heart. Her looks are tenderly kind in bestowing wealth; and at the same time they make our hearts free from all sorts of afflictions. My salutations to Bhudevi, sister-like to Sridevi. She blesses us with wisdom. She was graciously lifted up by the tusk of Lord Varaha (God who appeared in Boar form). She is the abode for saints who find joy in the scriptures. She makes the god-minded souls happy. I bow down again and again to Acarya Sri Madhva whose glory finds praise in the beginningless Vedas, who is without any flaw and is quite pure, whose intellect is the highest, always engaged with the memory of the Almighty Lord, whose glory is the subject matter of the Vedas. His form is quite enchanting and if fills one’s heart with the milk of kindness. Similarly, I offer my salutations to Bharati devi, fully endowed with female virtues, custodian of the principle of intellect, a bee of devotedness at the lotus feet of Sri Hari, the foremost among the souls of her kind and the bestower of opulence to the devotees of God.

My salutations to all spiritual masters who discard the evil-minded ones as enemies and help the good souls to sail across the ocean of life; and who bestow boons like the wish-fulfilling tree to those who serve them faithfully.

**THE BRAHMANDA (14-17)**

The Brahmanda or the hollow golden shell containing the universe is fully round in every direction, east to west or north to south. In it, twice its inner complement is Ghanodaka (solidified water) and the region of darkness is circular in shape, lying as it does within the Ghanodaka stretch. It is twice as wide as the circular earth which occupies the middle part. Upanisads describe the earth as ‘Prithvi’ - that which is spacious with abundance of wealth for living beings. The earth is said to stretch from sea to sea as the brahmin seers conceive it. It has the Meru mountain at its very centre and it contains seven islands and seven oceans stretching around the Meru centre. They are circular in form. The three together - earth, dark region and Ghanodaka - measure fifty crore yojanas from end to end. This is factual and not fictitious.

**DISTRIBUTION OF THE REMAINING ONE CRORE YOJANA (18-54)**

As detailed heretofore, 49 crore yojanas are distributed among the three regions, (7+14+28 = 49). The remaining one crore yo-jana should be converted into lakhs. Of the 100 lakh yojanas, 14 go to earth, 28 to the dark region and 56 to Ghanodaka. Thus, 98 lakh yojanas are distributed leaving a balance of 2 lakh yojanas. This should be converted into Thousands (two hundred thousand). Of these, 28 thousand for earth, 56 thousand for dark region and 112 thousand for Ghanodaka are distributed. 196 thousand being thus distributed, the remaining 4 thousand should be converted into hundreds (40 hundreds). The earth takes 5 hundred, the dark region 10, and Ghanodaka 20. Now the total is 35, leaving a balance of 5 hundred yojanas (500). This is distributed in the ratio of 70:140:280, making total of 490, with a balance of 10 yojanas. This 10 is distributed as 1:2:4; only yojanas now remain.

An intelligent person converts the yojanas into 12 krosas (1 yojana = 4 krosas) Now, the distribution is as follows -1 l/2:3:6.

Thus, 10 1/2 krosas having been distributed, 1 1/2 krosas still remain. The table of measurements being-1000 dandas = 1 krosa and 4 krosas = 1 yojana, 1 1/2 krosas means 1500 dandas (staff length). Distribution - earth 214, dark region 428, and Ghanodaka 856, thus making a total of 1498 dandas. The remaining 2 dandas convert themselves into 8 hastas (4 hastas or cubits = 1 danda). Distributing as 1:2:4 for the three regions respectively, the balance will be 1 hasta which is equal to 24 angulas (inches). Again, after distributing as 3:6:12, three inches still remain to be distributed. 3 Vrhis make 1 angula (Vrhi means a paddy seed). Therefore 3 angulas are equal to 9 vrhis. Distributing as before - 1:2:4, the length of 2 vrhis still remains to be distributed. One vrhi being equal to seven sarsapas (mustard seeds), now we are left with a stretch equal to 14 sarsapas put side by side. The distribution being as 2:4:8, no balance remains.

The Vedic mode of measurements being in this manner, measurements in all other respects must follow suit. Lord Sri Hari who came down incarnated as Sri Vyasa Maharsi, has thus shown the mode of distribution in the ratio of 1:2:4 - each unit being a seventh of the whole. Therefore the Puranas tell us that the hollow of Brah-manda measures exactly fifty crore yojanas. The fractional parts being added to 7,14, and 28, the total will be 50 crore.

The earth was created in the Krta yuga and therefore its measurements agree with the standards of that age. Lord Sri Maha Visnu created the earth during the first Krta yuga of the first ‘Kalpa’.

**THE TERRESTRIAL REGION (55-58)**

Basing our concept on the measurements indicated here-to-fore, the earth should be understood as consisting of the seven islands and the seven oceans, the middle most island Jambudvipa measuring one lakh yojanas, and each of the outer islands being twice as large as their inner complements. Similarly, the seven surrounding oceans too should be considered. Thus, the diameter of the circular earth measures 5 crore and 7 lakh yojanas up to the encircling land of gold, which itself is surrounded by the land of diamond.

The last two are known as ‘Antyadhyardha sthala’ This point will now be discussed.

**ANTYADHYARDHA STHALA (Land of gold and land of diamond)**

With reference to the location of Lokaloka parvata (which surrounds the entire terrestrial region) the measurement of earth other than ‘Brahma bhumi’ is said to be 6.08 crore yojanas. To reach the full measurement of 7.14 crore yojanas, we still require 1.06 crore yojanas. The measurement given for the region around Sud-dhodaka ocean being 1.28 crore yojanas, the land of gold should therefore measure 64 lakh yojanas on each side.

‘Adhyardha’ does not actually mean half; but it means ‘with a little addition to half. Therefore, a little more than half of 64 should be taken as 42 instead of 32 in order to satisfy the scriptural statement.

If this position is not maintained, the measurement of the Brah-manda interior would be more than 50 crore yojanas. If the Land of gold is taken to measure 96 lakh and the land of diamond, half of that - 48 lakh, then the terrestrial region would measure 5.07+0.96+0.96+0.48-1-0.48 = 7.95 or 8 crore yojanas. Following the ratio of 1:2:4, the Land of darkness will have to measure 16 crore and Ghanodaka, 32 crore. The total would be 56 crore yojanas; and this is entirely wrong.

What has been said herein about the Land of gold and Land of diamond is in agreement with the opinion of Acarya Purnaprajna. If the fringes of the earth were just earthy, it being prone to denudation, the measurement, given as exact to a mustard seed would not remain constant. Hence Lord Sri Hari has created the hard diamond land around.

**ANDHANTAMAS (Region of complete darkness) (79-83)**

The pit of darkness known as ‘Andhantamas’ is situated below the earth level, sunk in Ghanodaka. This region surrounds the Lo-kaloka parvata and is termed as ‘Adhogati’ or the downward path. Because ‘Andhantamas’ is below the level of Ghanodaka, it is possible for sinners to go down into it to some extent and to come up again. That they go down and come up has been stated by Sri Vyasa maharshi, the author of Brahma Sutras.

The principle of darkness extends even higher than the Lokaloka parvata which is 51 lakh yojanas high. It is petrified darkness like water turned into ice. If it were not so, where was the need for Sri Krsna, Lord of the Yadus, to create a tunnel through it by means of his Cakra (disc weapon)?

**THE NETHER WORLDS (93-98)**

Within the hollows of the earth down below are seven worlds ranged one below the other. They are Atala, Vitala, Sutala, Talata-la, Mahatala, Rasatala and Patala. Each of these is 10,000 yojanas in extent (width and height). Thus they occupy a total area of 70,000 yojanas. The Meru mountain has its base into the earth to the extent of 16,000 yojanas. The gaps between the seven nether worlds make a total of 3,000 yojanas (500 yojanas in each case). The earth extends still further below to a depth of 11 thousand yojanas; it is all rocky in nature. (16+70+3+11 = 100 thousand)

**ADI SESA (99-102)**

Adi Sesa is worshipped by all the serpents of Patala Ioka. He has his situation in the Ghanodaka down below; and he is immortal. He finds himself supported by ‘Vayu Kurma’ - Mukhya Prana in tortoise form Adi Sesa’s tail rests thereon. He is quite strong. Lifting one of his thousand hoods he supports the worlds above him so that the earth does not sink into Ghanodaka. Hence he is hailed as ‘Sarva Ioka dhurandharah”.

Adi Sesa by himself, is not competent to support the worlds. His strength is derived from the Supreme Lord ‘Samkarsana’, whom he worships in his heart. The space occupied by Adi Sesa in Ghanodaka is 99,000 yojanas.

**LOCATION OF HELLS (103-107)**

On the left hand side (south, in this instance) lower than the visible earth level, there is a big moat, many yojanas wide and deep. At its edge is “Samyamanipura,” god Yamadharma’s city, where he is always attending to his duties. There are thousands of his servants who are very cruel by appearance. The ‘Vaitarani’ river is here; and it is a terror to sinners. After crossing this river with a foretaste of the sufferings in store, they are taken down into hells like ‘Raurava’, which are horrible. Thousands of such hells are there for punishing sinners of different kinds. These hells are in the southern direction, at the level of the ‘Astadiggajas’ beneath Patala Ioka.

Another capital city of Yamadharma’s kingdom is also in Heaven. It is the harbinger of happiness to those who have done meritorious deeds here on earth.

**VISNU KURMA (106-109)**

Underneath the earth borne by Adi “Sesa, Ghanodaka extends to a depth of 24 crore, 99 lakh: yojanas, where it touches the golden shell of Brahmanda. That shell itself is 100 crore yojanas in thickness; and it rests on the back of Visnu Kurma, almost like an atom.

**THE UPPER WORLDS (110-123)**

Including the visible terrestrial world, the upper” worlds are seven in number, the seventh and the uppermost being “Satya Ioka.” The “Antariksa Ioka” begins one lakh yojanas above the earth. The ‘Svarga Ioka is at an altitude of 50 lakh yojanas. It is beautiful with divine Palatial buildings. Antariksa Ioka and Svarga Ioka are one crore yojanas breadthwise. Above Svarga Ioka is- ‘Mahar Ioka’, at an altitude of 75 lakh yojanas above the former. It is populated by Siddhas and rsis in large numbers. Above it is ‘Jana Ioka’ at an altitude of 11,250,000 yojanas. Then comes ‘Tapo Ioka', 16,875,000 higher up. This Ioka is fully populated with Siddhas and rsis; and provides abundance of amenities of all kinds. The three Iokas now spoken of - Maharloka, Janaloka and Tapoloka, have abodes beautified with varieties of gems in plenty. The extent of these three Iokas is similar, each measuring a third fraction of 50 crore 166,666,666 yojanas and 2 krosas.

Putting together the altitudes of all the Iokas spoken of and adding the gaps in between the adjacent Iokas, the total altitude will come to 40,625,000 yojanas. Having a similar distance in between, the ‘Satya Ioka’ begins. Therefore, it is at an altitude of 81,250,000 yojanas above the earth. That is where Brahma’s abode is situated. Its extent is similar to that of the three Iokas beneath it. The Satya Ioka itself has an altitude of 41,250,000 yojanas. (The total altitude of all the upper worlds will be 162,500,000 yojanas).

The Satya Ioka of Brahma is shaped like a tuber at its base. It is somewhat thin in the middle like a stalk and, at the top, it spreads out all round like the petals of a lotus flower. It is very very wide.

The Iokas of the universe, being all shaped to resemble lotus flowers, the whole thing looks like a huge lotus blooming upon the Ghanodaka.

**THE THREE ABODES OF VISNU (133-149)**

Upon the Ghanodaka on the northern side is the Abode of Visnu, known as ‘Anantasana’. It is Sridevi who is in the form of that Ioka where the released souls are. The learned say that it is at a height of one lakh yojanas. A similar Abode is in the milk ocean (Ksira Samudra) at an altitude of one and a half lakh yojanas with reference to its neighbouring earth. This Abode is called ‘Sveta dvipa.’ The three Abodes - Vaikunta, Sveta dvipa and Anantasana are only the forms of Sridevi. (They are non-material). They are thronged again and again with limitless numbers of released souls; and yet, they afford more and more accommodation. They are never completely filled up. Even a hall constructed by Visvakarma (the architect of gods) is able to accommodate limitless number of persons and things. Then, is it any wonder that the Abodes of Visnu are ever more and more accommodative? The belly of a sage (Sage Agastya) could contain the ocean. As such, the three Abodes of Visnu certainly possess wonderful capacity since they are no other than Maha Laksmi, the source of all abilities. During the period of ‘Maha Pralaya’ the released souls enter the womb of Visnu; and there too, they are happy since their non-material bodies are just the stuff of joy. During the period of creation, they return to the said Abodes. Their joy consists in praising the Lord’s glory - Joy Galore, prostrating at His feet and looking at His admirable form, often and often. They also enjoy different kinds of pleasures - of course, non-material.

The capital city of Vaikunta, Ayodhya by name, lies within seven fortifications and only the released souls enter into it. This city brightens the directions all around on account of its beautiful terraced quarters, adorned with wonderful gems. This city area is called ‘Parama Vyoma’ - non-material space. Around this city of Ayodhya flows the ‘Viraja’ river which rids the souls of the burdens of their causation bodies when they, take a dip in its waters. The river is like a moat encircling the city. No unreleased soul can ever cross it. Up to the seventh inner gate of the outer fortifications there is fear of bondage on account of the ‘Pradhana Vyoma’ (material sky) there. Within the bounds of the Viraja River, this sky is powerless. Hence it is declared in the Puranas that it does not exist there. Therefore, the Viraja River is said to flow between the material sky and the non - material sky. Brahma takes a dip in that river with the other eligible souls at the time of release from bondage. The causation bodies of all of them are got rid off; and then they get into the inner city, Ayodhya. Even Brahma goes only up to the seventh gate of the outer fortification before release; he does not enter the inner city. Then who else can?

**NINE OUTER ENVELOPES (150-152)**

The golden Brahmanda is the earthy envelope surrounding the universe. It is one hundred crore yojanas thick. Surrounding it are nine other envelopes, each ten times bigger than its inner complement. They are the envelopes made of the subtle material principles - water, fire air, sky, ego (ahamkara tattva), intellect (mahat tattva), Tamas, Rajas and Satva. The fourteen worlds of the universe are all contained at the core of these envelopes. Such is our wonderful universe. But, with all its envelopes, it is so tiny that it is not enough to occupy fully a single hair-pit on the body of Lord Sriman Narayana. Then, who else among the gods can stand comparison with Him? Let the learned ones find out.

**THE MERU MOUNTAIN (153-163)**

The golden Meru mountain is one lakh yojanas high. It is surrounded by the nine Khandas (Varsas of Jambu dvipa). It is the abode of Siddhas (souls who have attained a high degree of perfection). For all measurements concerning the universe, the Meru is the central point of reference. This mountain is thin at the base and goes on broadening gradually towards its top which is quite broad. The mountain resembles the ‘Unmatta’ flower. At the surface base it has a width of 16,000 yojanas and it goes down into the earth to the extent of 16,000yojanas. From the base up to ,the top, the Siddhas and yogis dwell.

At the top, the mountain has a width of 32,000 yojanas. It is round at the top and three sided at its base (like the cornered base of a nail). From the base to the top the mountain measures 84,000 yojanas. The circumference at the top is 96,000 yojanas (in round figures). There, in the middle, is the city of Brahma, known as ‘Sa-takaumbhi.’ Its palatial mansions are beautified with rare gems. The city is 10,000 yojanas long and equally broad.

There, the Gandharva chiefs, the prominent ones among the Sid-dhas, sages like Vasista and gods headed by Indra worship Brahma seated on the throne of gems. At the edges of the Meru top there are eight cities of the directional gods, one in each of the eight directions. The Meru mountain supports them all.

![рис-024.jpg|346](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-024--22e92683f73121d2.jpg>)

**THE RIVER GANGA (164-171)**

River Ganga pours down constantly in the open courtyard of Brahma’s palace such that he might use the waters for his ‘Sandhya vandana’ (worshipping Surya Narayana thrice a day - morning, afternoon and evening). The other gods there, also use it. By Brahma’s behest the river Ganga takes four courses to come down to earth from the Meru top and to purify the denizens here. Even now we can see it. The branch ‘Sita’ joins the sea (Lavana Samudra) on the east. ‘Alakananda’ pours into the southern sea and is quite famous as the purifier of the world. The branch ‘Caksu’ reaches the western sea and ‘Bhadra’ joins the northern sea. This river purifies those who use its waters in worshipful manner where ever they be in the four quarters (of Jambudvipa). The river was again brought to our earth in another form by the efforts of Bhagiratha. This river is called ‘Mandakini’ in the Himalayas. When it began to pour down in the Himalayan region, “Siva locked it up in the meshes of his knotted hair. (This was to mitigate the force with which Ganga came down; hence the name ‘Mandakini’). Being let down from the knotted hair and following Bhagiratha the fresh flow of waters joined the original course of the river; and after crossing the point at ‘Devaprayaga’, the river went down into the bowels of the earth to reach ‘Kapila asrama’ in the nether world to sanctify the ashes of .the 60,000 sons of Sagara cakravarti, burnt down by the angry look of Sage Kapila.

**ILAVRTA KHANDA (171 -183)**

This Khanda (Varsa) spreads around the Meru base which measures 8,000 yojanas from its centre, in every direction. There are four supporting mountains on each of the four sides around the base. These mountains are each 2,000 yojanas wide and 10,000 yojanas high. Beyond these mountains there is a stretch of 5,000 yojanas, and this makes up the 9,000 yojanas from the Meru base. The four supporting mountains are Mandara, Merumandara, Kumuda and Suparsva, in the four directions commencing from the east.

![рис-025.jpg|334](<../attachments/bhu-gola-tattva--%D1%80%D0%B8%D1%81-025--e2c781ffc73395e7.jpg>)

There are four gigantic trees on the four mountains - Cuta (mango), Jambu (rose apple tree), Plaksa (Indian fig) and Nyagrodha (banyan) respectively. Each of these trees is 100 yojanas high and the spread of the branches is to the extent of 1100 yojanas. The fruits of the trees resemble- mountain peaks. The ripe jambu fruits of the tree upon Merumandara (south) drop down and the juice flows as the Jambu river. The juice spreading on its banks, when dried up, becomes the finest gold which is known as ‘Jambunada’. The divine beings, who visit this region, drink the juice and make different kinds of ornaments for themselves out of ‘Jambunada’ (gold).

In this Varsa we find only the fair sex moving about day and night. No male person enters it for fear of Bhavanidevi’s curse. If any one trespasses, he is transformed into female form like Ila, the son of Manu. The female attendants of Gauridevi are one hundred million (‘arbuda’) in number. Siva, along with Gauridevi, worships the form of Lord Samkarsana, one of the ‘caturmurtis’ Vasudeva, Sam-karsana, Pradyumna and Aniruddha. Lord Samkarsana is the SUN brightening the hearts of yogis.

This Ilavrta Varsa is bounded by four mountains on the four sides. On the eastern side is the Gandhamadana mountain stretching between Nila and Nisadha mountains. It is 2000 yojanas in width. Of the same width, on the southern side, is the Nisadha mountain. It stretches from the eastern ocean up to the western. (This refers to the Lavana Samudra around Jambudvipa). On the western side is the Malyavan mountain, lying between Nila and Nisadha even like the Gandhamadana. On the northern side of Ilavrta is the Nila mountain, as wide and as long as Nisadha. Siddhas inhabit- these mountains.

**BHADRASVA KHANDA (183-193)**

The eastern part of Jambudvipa is occupied by only one Varsa, the Bhadrasva. It stretches up to the ocean on the east. Bhadrasravas, the son of Dharma, is there worshipping Lord Hayagriva; and he is the ruler of that Varsa. It is fully populated with his subjects. There are two other boundary mountains within the Khanda - Jata-ra and Devakuta, to the east of Gandhamadana. The two mountains extend between Nila and Nisadha, each being 2000 yojanas wide and 18A)00 yojanas long. Barring the space occupied by the three mountain ranges (Gandhamadana, Jatara and Devakuta) the Bhadrasva Varsa measures 27 thousand yojanas from west to east. Although Bhadrasva is a mentioned, have been created by the Omniscient Lord to maintain symmetry on all the four sides. A similar arrangement is there in the western Varsa. With the two additional mountain ranges the Bhadrasva Khanda is divided into three stretches, each nine thousand yojanas in width. It should be thus construed because it is said in the texts that each of the nine Khandas of Jambudvipa is 9000 yojanas in width. Symmetry is thus maintained.

**THE THREE SOUTHERN VARSAS (193-208)**

To the south of the mighty Nisadha mountain lies the Hari Varsa Khanda. It is 9,000 yojanas wide. Here the great devotee, Prahlada and the denizens of the Varsa worship the Narasimha form of the Supreme Lord. The boundary mountain on the south is Hemakuta, 2,000 yojanas wide and 10,000 yojanas high. (Its length is 10% less than that of Nisadha. Therefore, it is 90,000 yojanas in length).
